PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, Dring the Quarter ended September S0, 197.
182 CUSTOMS GAZ~ETTE. [JULY-SznT,* NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 308. HAVING reference to the &quot;Regulations for preventing Collisions at Sea,&quot; which came into force to-day, and which have been published in English and Chinese by the Statistical Department of the Imperial Maritime Customs, for general use in Chinese waters,- Notice is hereby given that their acceptance by China refers only to Chinese vessels of Foreign type, and that Chinese junks, fishing-boats, ete., will not exhibit the regulation lights. SHANGII, I8t TisY 1897. No. 309. CHEFOO DISTRICT. NOTICE is hereby given that a spar-buoy, painted in red and black vertical stripes, has been moored off the southern extremity of the sand spit, dry at low water, which extends to the southwvrd from Kung-tung-tao Island, and which is marked as &quot;East Sand Spit&quot; on the British Admiralty Chart (No. 1,26o) of Chefoo Harbour. Vessels must not attempt to pass between the buoy and the spit. SHANGHAI, Sth Augu8t 1897. No. 310. YANGTZE RIVER-CHINKIANG DISTRICT. COOPER BANK BEACON LIGHT DISCONTINUED. NoTIa is hereby given that the Cooper Bank Beacon Light, No. 61 in the published List for 1897, has been discontinued and replaced by a light-boat, exhibiting a fixed white light of the sixth order and moored on the north-eastern edge of the centre slibal of Cooper Bank. Vessels should pass to the northward of the light-boat. SHANGHAI, 7th September 1897.
1897] NOTIFICATION. 183 No. 311. CANTON DISTRICT. ROCK IN MALOWCHOW PASS. NOTICE is hereby given that a rocky patch, about two-thirds of a cable long, in a N.E. by E. and S.W. by S. direction, by about one-third of a cable wide, and having two pinnacles near its middle, on which there is a depth of not more than 3 feet at low water of spring tides, lies about half a cable south of mid-channel in the Malowehow Pass. From the two pinnacles of the patch the south end of Big Malowchow beirs N.E. ) N., and the centre of Oyster Rock, N. I W. A red and black chequered nun-buoy is moored about io fathoms to the E.N.E. of the two pinnacles, and vessels going through the pass should give the buoy a berth of not less than 50 yards. The passage north of the buoy is preferable. SHANGHAI, 17th Septembeir 1897.

APPENDIX . KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. JULY-SEPTEMBER 1897.
200 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [JULY-SE1 T-, (Beceived 16th October 1897.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 11T OCTOBER 1897. SIR, I.--a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistil Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the third quarter of the year 1897, July-September. b. The Custom House (I N) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two .Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Ik.TM 30 and Ik.7h 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsulmoon (g * ), Changehow (g j), Fotochow (A R N), and Kowloon (IL #a) stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word. &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II. 1897.] LAPPA (Received 2oth October 1897.) . CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, I1Ta OCToEn 1897. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the&quot;trade of this port for the third quarter of the year 1897, July-September. b. The Custom House (A [ ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.. 30 and Hk.M So per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by.the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom louse Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are knowli as the Malowchow (,W W ) and Chienshan (&#39;d (1t) stations, with three sub-stations, knowu as the Sbekkok (;6 A), Kuttai (&#39; t) and Kwanchiap (U ) sub-stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
